    16	    event = HaltEvent(
    17	        symbol="IDXX",
    18	        exchange="NASDAQ",
    19	        halt_date="2026-05-05",
    20	        halt_time="08:00:00",
    21	        reason_code="T1",
    22	        reason_description="News Pending",
    23	        name="IDEXX Laboratories Inc",
    24	        last_price=563.12,
    25	        source="nasdaq_rss",
    26	    )
    27	    assert event.symbol in universe
    28	    meta = universe.get(event.symbol)
    29	    rendered = render_halt(
    30	        event,
    31	        sector=meta.sector,
    32	        subsector=meta.subsector,
    33	        name_override=meta.name,
    34	    )
    35	    assert "SA: IDXX" in rendered
    36	    assert "halted at $563.12" in rendered
    37	    assert "Sector: Healthcare Services" in rendered
    38	
    39	
    40	def test_synthetic_resume_renders():
    41	    universe = Universe()
    42	    event = HaltEvent(
    43	        symbol="IDXX",
    44	        exchange="NASDAQ",
    45	        halt_date="2026-05-05",
    46	        halt_time="08:00:00",
    47	        reason_code="T1",
    48	        reason_description="News Pending",
    49	        name="IDEXX Laboratories Inc",
    50	        last_price=563.12,
    51	        resume_trade_time="08:30:00",
    52	        source="nasdaq_rss",
    53	    )
    54	    meta = universe.get(event.symbol)
    55	    rendered = render_resume(
    56	        event,
    57	        sector=meta.sector,
    58	        subsector=meta.subsector,
    59	        name_override=meta.name,
    60	    )
    61	    assert "SA: IDXX [shares to resume trading at 08:30 ET]" in rendered
    62	    assert "originally halted at 08:00 ET" in rendered
    63	
    64	
    65	def test_halt_tracker_emits_halt_then_resume_for_full_cycle():
    66	    """Simulate the full feed-poll cycle: halt event observed, then resume info added."""
    67	    tracker = HaltTracker()
    68	    halt_only = HaltEvent(
    69	        symbol="ARVN",
    70	        exchange="NASDAQ",
    71	        halt_date="2026-05-01",
    72	        halt_time="11:23:00",
    73	        reason_code="T1",
    74	        reason_description="News Pending",
    75	        source="nasdaq_rss",
    76	    )
    77	    halt_with_resume = HaltEvent(
    78	        symbol="ARVN",
    79	        exchange="NASDAQ",
    80	        halt_date="2026-05-01",
    81	        halt_time="11:23:00",
    82	        reason_code="T1",
    83	        reason_description="News Pending",
    84	        resume_trade_time="11:35:00",
    85	        source="nasdaq_rss",
    86	    )
    87	    # First poll: just the halt
    88	    out_first = list(tracker.ingest([halt_only]))
    89	    assert [k for k, _ in out_first] == ["halt"]
    90	    # Second poll: same halt re-fetched
    91	    out_second = list(tracker.ingest([halt_only]))
    92	    assert out_second == []
    93	    # Third poll: resume info now populated
    94	    out_third = list(tracker.ingest([halt_with_resume]))
    95	    assert [k for k, _ in out_third] == ["resume"]
    96	    # Fourth poll: still seeing the resumed event — no further emit
    97	    out_fourth = list(tracker.ingest([halt_with_resume]))
    98	    assert out_fourth == []

     1	"""Halt-reason-code → human-readable description.
     2	
     3	Authoritative source: https://www.nasdaqtrader.com/trader.aspx?id=tradehaltcodes
     4	NYSE halt-reason strings observed empirically from
     5	https://www.nyse.com/api/trade-halts/current/download.
     6	
     7	Phase 1 default filter (per phase1-data-sources.md §3): include T1, T2, T5, T6,
     8	T12, H4, H9, H10, H11, M1, LUDP, LUDS, MWC1/2/3, MWCO, MWCQ. Exclude T8, M2,
     9	O1, IPO* by default but log them.
    10	
    11	The values here are intentionally short — they appear inside the halt
    12	notification rendered in `template.render_halt()`, where space matters.
    13	"""
    14	from __future__ import annotations
    15	
    16	# Nasdaq's published reason codes (the canonical industry list — used by NYSE
    17	# too via the consolidated tape). Keys are the literal codes as published in
    18	# Nasdaq Trader's RSS feed. Values are short human descriptions.
    19	NASDAQ_REASON_CODES: dict[str, str] = {
    20	    "T1": "News Pending",
    21	    "T2": "News Released",
    22	    "T5": "Single Stock Trading Pause (10% in 5 min)",
    23	    "T6": "Extraordinary Market Activity",
    24	    "T8": "ETP / EXTL halt",
    25	    "T12": "Additional Information Requested",
    26	    "H4": "Non-Compliance with Listing Requirements",
    27	    "H9": "Not Current with Filings",
    28	    "H10": "SEC Trading Suspension",
    29	    "H11": "Regulatory Concern (other market)",
    30	    "M1": "Corporate Action",
    31	    "M2": "Quotation Not Available",
    32	    "O1": "Operations Halt",
    33	    "IPO1": "IPO Issue Not Yet Trading",
    34	    "IPOQ": "IPO Quotation Initiated",
    35	    "IPO2": "IPO Quotation",
    36	    "LUDP": "LULD Pause",
    37	    "LUDS": "LULD Trading Pause - Straddle Condition",
    38	    "MWC1": "Market-Wide Circuit Breaker Level 1",
    39	    "MWC2": "Market-Wide Circuit Breaker Level 2",
    40	    "MWC3": "Market-Wide Circuit Breaker Level 3",
    41	    "MWCO": "MWCB Order halt",
    42	    "MWCQ": "MWCB Quote halt",
    43	    "R4": "Resumption Trade Time",
    44	    "R1": "Resumption Quote Time",
    45	}
    46	
    47	# NYSE's API publishes "Reason" as a free-text string (e.g. "News pending",
    48	# "News Released", "LULD pause"). Map common strings back to the canonical
    49	# Nasdaq code so dedup by (symbol, halt_date, halt_time) works across feeds.
    50	NYSE_REASON_TO_CODE: dict[str, str] = {
    51	    "news pending": "T1",
    52	    "news released": "T2",
    53	    "luld pause": "LUDP",
    54	    "luld trading pause": "LUDP",
    55	    "regulatory halt": "H11",
    56	    "single stock trading pause": "T5",
    57	    "extraordinary market activity": "T6",
    58	    "operations halt": "O1",
    59	    "sec trading suspension": "H10",
    60	    "non-compliance": "H4",
    61	    "additional information requested": "T12",
    62	    "ipo issue not yet trading": "IPO1",
    63	    "ipo quotation": "IPOQ",
    64	    "corporate action": "M1",
    65	    "market-wide circuit breaker level 1": "MWC1",
    66	    "market-wide circuit breaker level 2": "MWC2",
    67	    "market-wide circuit breaker level 3": "MWC3",
    68	}
    69	
    70	# Phase 1 emit-by-default filter (codes that fire alerts). T8, M2, O1, IPO*
    71	# are excluded by default but still logged.
    72	PHASE1_EMIT_CODES: set[str] = {
    73	    "T1", "T2", "T5", "T6", "T12",
    74	    "H4", "H9", "H10", "H11",
    75	    "M1",
    76	    "LUDP", "LUDS",
    77	    "MWC1", "MWC2", "MWC3", "MWCO", "MWCQ",
    78	}
    79	
    80	
    81	def describe(code: str) -> str:
    82	    """Return human-readable description for a halt code, or the code itself
    83	    if not in the table (so unknown codes don't drop information)."""
    84	    return NASDAQ_REASON_CODES.get(code.upper(), code)
    85	
    86	
    87	def normalize_nyse_reason(reason: str) -> str:
    88	    """Map a free-text NYSE reason string to the canonical Nasdaq code.
    89	    Returns the original string upper-cased if no mapping is found."""
    90	    key = (reason or "").strip().lower()
    91	    return NYSE_REASON_TO_CODE.get(key, reason.upper())
    92	
    93	
    94	def is_phase1_emit_code(code: str) -> bool:
    95	    """Whether a halt-reason code should fire a Slack alert in Phase 1."""
    96	    return code.upper() in PHASE1_EMIT_CODES

codex
**High**

[halt_monitor.py](/mnt/c/Users/jroyp/Dropbox/Claude%20Folder/sa-monitor/src/halt_monitor.py:251): Bug-3 pre-mark can suppress a legitimate standalone follow-up because it uses `find_followup_news()`’s 0-60 minute post-halt window, while the halt alert’s cross-ref note only uses `NewsCache.lookup()`’s default +5 minute lookforward via `_cross_ref_note()`.

One-line repro: halt at `07:00 ET`, PR already cached at `07:10 ET`; `_emit()` renders the halt with no “Follows … press release” note, then lines 251-254 still add the halt to `followed_up`, so `_emit_followups()` emits `0`.

I confirmed this with an in-memory repro:
`followed_after_emit=True`, `cross_ref_count=0`, `followups_emitted=0`.

The pre-mark should only run when the resolving PR was actually conveyed by the halt alert, not merely when it is already present in the wider follow-up window. Targeted pytest could not run here because the sandbox has no writable temp directory for pytest capture.

[halt_monitor.py](/mnt/c/Users/jroyp/Dropbox/Claude%20Folder/sa-monitor/src/halt_monitor.py:251): Bug-3 pre-mark can suppress a legitimate standalone follow-up because it uses `find_followup_news()`’s 0-60 minute post-halt window, while the halt alert’s cross-ref note only uses `NewsCache.lookup()`’s default +5 minute lookforward via `_cross_ref_note()`.

One-line repro: halt at `07:00 ET`, PR already cached at `07:10 ET`; `_emit()` renders the halt with no “Follows … press release” note, then lines 251-254 still add the halt to `followed_up`, so `_emit_followups()` emits `0`.

I confirmed this with an in-memory repro:
`followed_after_emit=True`, `cross_ref_count=0`, `followups_emitted=0`.

The pre-mark should only run when the resolving PR was actually conveyed by the halt alert, not merely when it is already present in the wider follow-up window. Targeted pytest could not run here because the sandbox has no writable temp directory for pytest capture.
